Cutoff Boltzmann equation with polynomial perturbation near Maxwellian Thanks: C. Cao: Yau Mathematical Science Center and Beijing Institute of Mathematical Sciences and Applications, Tsinghua University, Beijing, 100084, P. R. China. Email address: chuqicao@gmail.com
Chuqi Cao

Abstract
. In this paper, we consider the cutoff Boltzmann equation near Maxwellian, we proved the global existence and uniqueness for the cutoff Boltzmann equation in polynomial weighted space for all γ ∈ ( − 3 , 1 ] \gamma\in(-3,1] . We also proved initially polynomial decay for the large velocity in L 2 L^{2} space will induce polynomial decay rate, while initially exponential decay will induce exponential rate for the convergence. Our proof is based on newly established inequalities for the cutoff Boltzmann equation and semigroup techniques. Moreover, by generalizing the L x ∞ ​ L v 1 ∩ L x , v ∞ L_{x}^{\infty}L^{1}_{v}\cap L^{\infty}_{x,v} approach, we prove the global existence and uniqueness of a mild solution to the Boltzmann equation with bounded polynomial weighted L x , v ∞ L^{\infty}_{x,v} norm under some small condition on the initial L x 1 ​ L v ∞ L^{1}_{x}L^{\infty}_{v} norm and entropy so that this initial data allows large amplitude oscillations. Keywords: Boltzmann equation; Global existence; Polynomial weighted space; Convergence to equilibrium. AMS subject classifications: 35B40, 35Q20, 47D06.



1.
Introduction

The Boltzmann equation reads

(1)

\partial_{t}F+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F=Q(F,F),\quad F(0,x,v)=F_{0}(x,v),

where
F(t,x,v)\geq 0
is a distributional functions of colliding particles which, at time
t>0
and position
x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{3}
, move with velocity
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
. We remark that the Boltzmann equation is one of the fundamental equations of mathematical physics and is a cornerstone of statistical physics. The Boltzmann collision operator
Q
is a bilinear operator which acts only on the velocity variable
v
, that is

Q(G,F)(v)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(v-v_{*},\sigma)(G^{\prime}_{*}F^{\prime}-G_{*}F)d\sigma dv_{*}.

Let us give some explanations on the collision operator.

(1)

We use the standard shorthand
F=F(v),G_{*}=G(v_{*}),F^{\prime}=F(v^{\prime}),G^{\prime}_{*}=G(v^{\prime}_{*})
, where
v^{\prime},v^{\prime}_{*}
are given by

v^{\prime}=\frac{v+v_{*}}{2}+\frac{|v-v_{*}|}{2}\sigma,\quad v_{*}^{\prime}=\frac{v+v_{*}}{2}-\frac{|v-v_{*}|}{2}\sigma,\quad\sigma\in\mathbb{S}^{2}.

This representation follows the parametrization of set of solutions of the physical law of elastic collision:

v+v_{*}=v^{\prime}+v^{\prime}_{*},\quad|v|^{2}+|v_{*}|^{2}=|v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v^{\prime}_{*}|^{2}.

(2)

The nonnegative function
B(v-v_{*},\sigma)
in the collision operator is called the Boltzmann collision kernel. It is always assumed to depend only on
|v-v_{*}|
and the deviation angle
\theta
through
\cos\theta:=\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot\sigma
.

(3)

In the present work, our
basic assumptions on the kernel
B
can be concluded as follows:

\mathbf{(A1).}

The Boltzmann kernel
B
takes the form:
B(v-v_{*},\sigma)=|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot\sigma)
, where
b
is a nonnegative function.

\mathbf{(A2).}

The angular function
b(\cos\theta)
satisfies the Grad’s cutoff assumption

K\leq b(\cos\theta)\leq K^{-1},\quad K>0.

\mathbf{(A3).}

The parameter
\gamma
satisfies the condition
-3<\gamma\leq 1
.

\mathbf{(A4).}

Without lose of generality, we may assume that
B(v-v_{*},\sigma)
is supported in the set
0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2
, i.e.
\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot\sigma\geq 0
, otherwise
B
can be replaced by its symmetrized form:

\overline{B}(v-v_{*},\sigma)=|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\big(b(\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot\sigma)+b(\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot(-\sigma))\big)\mathrm{1}_{\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot\sigma\geq 0},

where
\mathrm{1}_{A}
is the characteristic function of the set
A
.

Remark 1.1
.

Generally, the case
\gamma>0
,
\gamma=0
, and
\gamma<0
correspond to so-called hard, Maxwellian, and soft potentials respectively.

1.1.
Basic properties and the perturbation equation

We recall some basic facts on the Boltzmann equation.

\bullet

Conservation Law.
Formally if
F
is the solution to the Boltzmann equation (
1
) with initial data
F_{0}
, then it enjoys the conservation of mass, momentum and the energy, that is,

(2)

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{3}}F(t,x,v)\varphi(v)dvdx=0,\quad\varphi(v)=1,v,|v|^{2}.

For simplicity, we introduce the normalization identities on the initial data
F_{0}
which satisfies

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}F_{0}(x,v)\,dvdx=1,\quad\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}F_{0}(x,v)\,v\,dvdx=0,\quad\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}F_{0}(x,v)\,|v|^{2}\,dvdx=3.

This means that the equilibrium associated to (
1
) will be the standard Gaussian function, i.e.

\mu(v):=(2\pi)^{-3/2}e^{-|v|^{2}/2},

which enjoys the same mass, momentum and energy as
F_{0}
. By the Boltzmann H-Theorem, the solution to the Boltzmann equation (
1
) satisfies

(3)

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}F(t,x,v)\ln F(t,x,v)dvdx\leq\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}F_{0}(x,v)\ln F_{0}(x,v)dvdx,\quad\forall t\geq 0.

\bullet

Perturbation Equation.
In the perturbation framework, let
f
be the perturbation such that

F=\mu+f.

The Boltzmann equation (
1
) becomes

(4)

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=Q(\mu,f)+Q(f,\mu)+Q(f,f):=Lf+Q(f,f),

with the linearized operator
L=Q(\mu,\cdot)+Q(\cdot,\mu)
.

1.2.
Brief review of previous results

In what follows we recall some known results on the Landau and Boltzmann equations with a focus on the topics under consideration in this paper, particularly on global existence and large-time behavior of solutions to the spatially inhomogeneous equations in the perturbation framework. For global solutions to the renormalized equation with large initial data, we mention the classical works
[
14
,
15
,
40
,
50
,
51
,
13
,
6
]
. For the stability of vacuum, see
[
41
,
30
,
12
]
for the Landau, cutoff and non-cutoff Boltzmann equation with moderate soft potential respectively.

We focus on the results in the perturbation framework. In the near Maxwellian framework, a key point is to characterize the dissipation property in the
L^{2}
norm in
v
for the linearized operator and further control the trilinear term in an appropriate way. More precisely, for the cutoff Boltzmann equation, the corresponding linearized operator
L_{\mu}
is self-adjoint and has the null space

L_{\mu}f=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}(Q(\mu,\sqrt{\mu}f)+Q(\sqrt{\mu}f,\mu)),\quad\mbox{Null}(L_{\mu})=\mbox{Span}\{\sqrt{\mu},\sqrt{\mu}v,\sqrt{\mu}|v|^{2}\},

and having the coercivity property

\quad(f,L_{\mu}f)\leq-C\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}},\quad\forall f\in\hbox{Null}(L_{\mu})^{\perp},

for some constant
C>0
, such coercivity property is essential in the near Maxwellian framework.

In the near Maxwellian framework, global existence and large-time behavior of solutions to the spatially inhomogeneous equations is proved in
[
27
,
28
,
46
,
47
]
for the cutoff Boltzmann equation and in
[
26
]
for the Landau equation. For the non-cutoff Boltzmann equation it is proved in
[
2
,
3
,
4
,
5
,
23
,
24
]
, see also
[
19
]
for a recent work. We also refer to
[
31
,
32
,
33
,
18
,
17
,
20
]
for the Vlasov-Poisson/Maxwell-Boltzmann/Landau equation near Maxwellian. We remark here all these works above are based on the following decomposition

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=L_{\mu}f+\Gamma(f,f),\quad L_{\mu}f=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}(Q(\mu,\sqrt{\mu}f)+Q(\sqrt{\mu}f,\mu)),\quad\Gamma(f,f)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}Q(\sqrt{\mu}f,\sqrt{\mu}f),

which means the result are in
\mu^{-1/2}
weighted space.

In the near Maxwellian framework, there are also several results for
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
well-posedness results near Maxwellian. For the cutoff Boltzmann equation near Maxwellian, a
L^{2}-L^{\infty}
approach has been introduced in
[
48
,
29
]
and apply to various contexts, see
[
38
,
35
]
and the reference therein for example. Global
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
well-posedness result near Maxwellian is proved for the Landau equation in
[
37
]
and for the non-cutoff Boltzmann equation in
[
8
,
45
]
.
The solution with large amplitude initial data is first proved in
[
16
]
under the assumption of small entropy, we also refer to
[
21
]
for the Boltzmann equation with large amplitude initial data in bounded domains and
[
52
]
for the relativistic Boltzmann equation with large amplitude initial data.

For inhomogeneous equations with polynomial weighted perturbation near Maxwellian, in Gualdani-Mischler-Mouhot
[
25
]
the authors first prove the global existence and large-time behavior of solutions with polynomial velocity weight for the cutoff Boltzmann equation with hard potential in
L^{1}_{v}L^{\infty}_{x}(1+|v|^{k}),k>2
, this method is generalized to the Landau equation in
[
10
,
11
]
. The non-cutoff Boltzmann equation with hard potential is proved in
[
36
,
7
]
, the soft potential case is proved in
[
9
]
. The cutoff Boltzmann equation with soft potential is proved in this paper.

1.3.
Main results and notations

Let us first introduce the function spaces and notations.

\bullet
For any
p\in[1,+\infty)
,
q\in{\mathbb{R}}
the
L^{p}_{q}
norm is defined by

\|f\|_{L^{p}_{q}}^{p}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f(v)|^{p}\langle v\rangle^{pq}dv,

where the Japanese bracket
\langle v\rangle
is defined as
\langle v\rangle:=(1+|v|^{2})^{1/2}
.

\bullet
For any
p\in[1,+\infty)
,
k\in{\mathbb{R}},a>0,b\in(0,2)
the
L^{p}_{k,a,b}
norm is defined by

\|f\|_{L^{p}_{k,a,b}}^{p}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f(v)|^{p}\langle v\rangle^{pq}e^{pa\langle v\rangle^{b}}dv.

\bullet
For real numbers
m,l
, we define the weighted Sobolev space
H_{l}^{m}
by

H^{m}_{l}:=\{f(v)||f|_{H^{m}_{l}}=|\langle\cdot\rangle^{l}\langle D\rangle^{m}f|_{L^{2}}<+\infty\},

where
a(D)
is a pseudo-differential operator with the symbol
a(\xi)
and it is defined as

(a(D)f)(v):=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{i(v-u)\xi}a(\xi)f(u)dud\xi,

and we denote
H^{m}:=H^{m}_{0}
. The weighted Sobolev space
H^{m}_{k,a,b}
can be defined in a similar way.

\bullet
For function
f(x,v),x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{3},v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, the norm
\|\cdot\|_{H^{\alpha}_{x}H^{m}_{l}}
is defined as

\|f\|_{H^{\alpha}_{x}H^{m}_{l}}:=\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\langle D_{x}\rangle^{\alpha}f(x,\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{m}_{l}}dx\right)^{1/2}.

If
\alpha=0
,
H^{0}_{x}H^{m}_{l}=L^{2}_{x}H^{m}_{l}
. The weighted Sobolev space
H^{\alpha}_{x}H^{m}_{k,a,b}
can be defined in a similar way.

\bullet
We write
a\lesssim b
indicate that there is a uniform constant
C
, which may be different on different lines, such that
a\leq Cb
. We use the notation
a\sim b
whenever
a\lesssim b
and
b\lesssim a
. We denote
C_{a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{n}}
by a constant depending on parameters
a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{n}
. Moreover, we use parameter
\epsilon
to represent different positive numbers much less than 1 and determined in different cases.

\bullet
We use
(f,g)
to denote the inner product of
f,g
in the
v
variable
(f,g)_{L^{2}_{v}}
for short, we use
(f,g)_{L^{2}_{k}}
to denote
(f,g\langle v\rangle^{2k})_{L^{2}_{v}}
.

\bullet
For any function
f
we define

\|f(\theta)\|_{L^{1}_{\theta}}:=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f(\theta)d\sigma=2\pi\int_{0}^{\pi}f(\theta)\sin\theta d\theta.

\bullet
Gamma function and Beta function are defined by

\Gamma(x):=\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{x-1}e^{-t}dt,\quad x>0,\quad B(p,q):=\int_{0}^{1}t^{p-1}(1-t)^{q-1}dt=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{p-1}}{(1+t)^{p+q}},\quad p,q>0.

We recall that Beta and Gamma functions fulfill the following properties:

(5)

B(p,q)=\frac{\Gamma(p)\Gamma(q)}{\Gamma(p+q)},\quad B(p,q)\sim q^{-p}\quad\hbox{if}\quad 0<p\leq 2.

\bullet
For the cross section
B(\cos\theta,|v-v_{*}|)
with an angular cutoff, we will use the notation
Q^{\pm}
defined as

(6)

Q^{+}(f,g)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(\cos\theta,|v_{*}-v|)f_{*}^{\prime}g^{\prime}dv_{*}d\sigma,\quad Q^{-}(f,g)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(\cos\theta,|v-v_{*}|)f_{*}gdv_{*}d\sigma.

Note that
Q(f,g)=Q^{+}(f,g)-Q^{-}(f,g)
.

\bullet
For the linearized operator
L
we have

\ker(L)=\text{span}\{\mu,v_{1}\mu,v_{2}\mu,v_{3}\mu,|v|^{2}\mu\}.

We define the projection onto
\ker(L)
by

(7)

Pf:=\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}fdvdx\right)\mu+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}v_{i}fdvdx\right)v_{i}\mu+\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{\sqrt{6}}fdvdx\right)\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{\sqrt{6}}\mu.

\bullet
For any
k\in{\mathbb{R}},\gamma\in(-3,1]
, we define

\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}^{2}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\mu(v_{*})|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f(v)|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdv_{*}.

It is easily seen that
\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}\sim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}}
.

\bullet
During the whole paper, we will denote
N
by

(8)

N=\left\{\begin{aligned} &2,&\quad\gamma\in(-\frac{3}{2},1],\\
&3,&\quad\gamma\in(-\frac{5}{2},-\frac{3}{2}],\\
&4,&\quad\gamma\in(-3,-\frac{5}{2}].\end{aligned}\right.

\bullet
We let the multi-indices
\alpha
and
\beta
be
\alpha=[\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}]
,
\beta=[\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}]
and define

\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}:=\partial^{\alpha_{1}}_{x_{1}}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{x_{2}}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{x_{3}}\partial^{\beta_{1}}_{v_{1}}\partial^{\beta_{2}}_{v_{2}}\partial^{\beta_{3}}_{v_{3}}.

\bullet

If each component of
\theta
is not greater than that of the
\overline{\theta}
’s, we denote by
\theta\leq\overline{\theta}
;

\theta<\overline{\theta}
means
\theta\leq\overline{\theta}
, and
|\theta|<|\overline{\theta}|
.

\bullet
For any
k\in{\mathbb{R}},a>0,b\in(0,2)
, if
\gamma\in(-\frac{3}{2},1]
we will denote

(9)

\|f\|_{X_{k}}^{2}:=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\|\langle v\rangle^{k}\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2},\quad\|f\|_{X_{k,a,b}}^{2}:=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\|\partial^{\alpha}f\langle v\rangle^{k}e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}.

If
-3<\gamma\leq-\frac{3}{2}
we will denote the weight function
w(\alpha,\beta)
as

(10)

w(|\alpha|,|\beta|)=\langle v\rangle^{k-a|\alpha|-b|\beta|+c},\quad b=7\max\{-\gamma,0\},\quad a=b+\min\{\gamma,0\}=6\max\{-\gamma,0\},\quad c=4b.

Note that
w(\alpha,\beta)=w(|\alpha|,|\beta|)
, the two notations will have the same meaning in the whole paper. We then define

(11)

\|f\|_{X_{k}}^{2}:=\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N}C^{2}_{|\alpha|,|\beta|}\|w(\alpha,\beta)\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2},\quad\|f\|_{X_{k,a,b}}^{2}:=\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N}C_{|\alpha|,|\beta|}^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}fw(\alpha,\beta)e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2},

where the constant
C_{|\alpha|,|\beta|}
satisfies

(12)

C_{|\alpha|,|\beta|}\ll C_{|\alpha|,|\beta_{1}|},\quad\forall|\alpha|\geq 0,\quad 0\leq|\beta_{1}|<|\beta|,\quad C_{|\alpha|+1,||\beta|-1}\gg C_{|\alpha|,|\beta|},\quad\forall|\alpha|\geq 0,\quad|\beta|\geq 1,

and we will denote
Y_{k}:=X_{k+\gamma/2},Y_{k,a,b}:=X_{k+\gamma/2,a,b}
. We also define

(13)

\|f\|_{Y_{k,*}}^{2}:=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}^{2},\quad\gamma\in(-\frac{3}{2},1],\quad\|f\|_{Y_{k,*}}^{2}:=\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N}C^{2}_{|\alpha|,|\beta|}\|w(\alpha,\beta)\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{\gamma/2,*}}^{2},\quad\gamma\in(-3,-\frac{3}{2}].

\bullet
Define
\bar{X_{0}}:=H^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
if
\gamma\in(-\frac{3}{2},1]
,
\bar{X_{0}}:=H^{N}_{x,v}
if
\gamma\in(-3,-\frac{3}{2}]
.

\bullet
Define the relative entropy by

(14)

H(F(t)):=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}F(t,x,v)\ln F(t,x,v)-\mu\ln\mu dvdx,

it is easily seen that
H(F(t))\geq 0,\forall t\geq 0,H(\mu)=0
.

1.4.
Main results

We may now state our main results.

Theorem 1.2
.

Consider the Cauchy problem

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=Lf+Q(f,f),\quad\mu+f\geq 0,\quad f(0)=f_{0},\quad Pf_{0}=0.

•

Polynomial case: For any
k\geq 6
, there exists a small constant
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that for any initial data
f_{0}\in X_{k}
satisfies

\mu+f_{0}\geq 0,\quad Pf_{0}=0,\quad\|f_{0}\|_{X_{6}}<\epsilon_{0},\quad\|f_{0}\|_{X_{k}}<+\infty,

there exists a unique global solution
f\in L^{\infty}([0,+\infty),X_{k})
satisfies
F=\mu+f\geq 0
. Moreover if
\gamma\in[0,1]
, we have

\|f(t)\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim e^{-\lambda t}\|f_{0}\|_{X_{k}},\quad\forall t\in(0,+\infty),

for some constant
\lambda>0
. If
\gamma<0
, then for any
6\leq k_{1}<k
we have

\|f(t)\|_{X_{k_{1}}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{|k-k_{*}|}{|\gamma|}}\|f_{0}\|_{X_{k}},\quad\forall k_{*}\in(k_{1},k),\quad\forall t\in(0,+\infty).

•

Exponential case: For any
k\in{\mathbb{R}},a>0,b\in(0,2)
, there exists a small constant
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
f_{0}\in X_{k,a,b}
satisfies

\mu+f_{0}\geq 0,\quad Pf_{0}=0,\quad\|f_{0}\|_{X_{k,a,b}}<\epsilon_{0},

there exists a unique global solution
f\in L^{\infty}([0,+\infty),X_{k,a,b})
satisfies
F=\mu+f\geq 0
. Moreover if
\gamma\in[0,1]
, we have

\|f(t)\|_{X_{k,a,b}}\lesssim e^{-\lambda t}\|f_{0}\|_{X_{k,a,b}},\quad\forall t\in(0,+\infty),

for some constant
\lambda>0
. If
\gamma<0
, then for any
0<a_{0}<a
we have

\|f(t)\|_{X_{k,a_{0},b}}\lesssim e^{-\lambda t^{\frac{b}{b-\gamma}}}\|f_{0}\|_{X_{k,a,b}},\quad\forall t\in(0,+\infty),

for some constant
\lambda>0
.

Several comments on the results are in order:

\bullet
The choice of
N
is optimal in the sense that
N-2
is the minimal integer such that

\|Q(f,f)\|_{H^{N-2}_{v}}\leq C\|f\|_{H^{N-2}_{4}}^{2}.

\bullet
We emphasize that for global solutions in the polynomial weight space, we only need the small initial value of a given norm, more precisely we only require the smallness of
X_{6}
instead of
X_{k}
.

By assuming the entropy is small we can prove the well-posedness for the Boltzmann equation with large amplitude initial data.

Theorem 1.3
.

Consider the Cauchy problem

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=Lf+Q(f,f),\quad\mu+f_{0}\geq 0,\quad f(0)=f_{0},\quad Pf_{0}=0.

For any
\gamma\in(-3,1]
, there exists a constant
k_{0}\geq 8
such that for all
k\geq k_{0}
, for any
\beta\geq\max\{3,3+\gamma\}
, for any fixed constant
M>1
, there exists a constant
\epsilon_{0}>0
depends on
M,k,\beta
such that if

(15)

\|\langle v\rangle^{k+\beta}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq M,\quad H(F_{0})+\|\langle v\rangle^{k}f_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L_{v}^{\infty}}\leq\epsilon_{0},

then the Boltzmann solution has a unique global mild solution
f\in L^{\infty}([0,+\infty),L^{\infty}_{x,v}(\langle v\rangle^{k+\beta}))
satisfies that

\|\langle v\rangle^{k+\beta}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq CM^{2},\quad\forall t\geq 0,

where
C>0
depends on
\gamma,k,\beta
. Moreover, for the case
\gamma\geq 0
we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{k+\beta}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq Ce^{-\lambda t},\quad\forall t\geq 0,

for some constants
C,\lambda>0
. For the case
-3<\gamma<0
, if we further assume
\beta\geq 6
, for any
r\in(0,1)
we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{k}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C(1+t)^{-r},\quad\forall t\geq 0,

for some constants
C>0
.

\bullet

Comment on the solutions.
It should be pointed out that initial data satisfying the smallness condition (
15
) are allowed to have large amplitude oscillations in the spatial variable. For instance, one may take

F_{0}(x,v)=\rho_{0}(x)\mu(v),\quad x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3},

with
\rho_{0}(x)\geq 0,\rho_{0}\in L^{\infty}_{x},\rho_{0}-1\in L^{1}_{x},\rho_{0}\ln\rho_{0}-\rho_{0}+1\in L^{1}_{x}
. It is easy to verify that (
15
) holds if
\|\rho_{0}\ln\rho_{0}-\rho_{0}+1\|_{L^{1}_{x}}+\|\rho_{0}-1\|_{L^{1}_{x}}
is small. Even though
\|\rho_{0}\ln\rho_{0}-\rho_{0}+1\|_{L^{1}_{x}}+\|\rho_{0}-1\|_{L^{1}_{x}}
is required to be small, initial data are allowed to have large amplitude oscillations.

1.5.
Strategies and ideas of the proof

In this subsection, we will explain main strategies and ideas of the proof for our results.

We briefly talk on the semigroup method, this method is first initiated in
[
43
]
and extended into an abstract setting in a famous work by Gualdani-Mischler-Mouhot
[
25
]
, see also its application in kinetic Fokker-Planck equation in
[
42
]
. The main idea for the
L^{2}
case can be expressed briefly as follows: Taking the case
\gamma=0
in the homogeneous Boltzmann equation for example, first by existing results we have

(Lf,f)_{L^{2}(\mu^{-1/2})}\leq-\lambda\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mu^{-1/2})},\quad\|S_{L}(t)f_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mu^{-1/2})}\leq e^{-\lambda t}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mu^{-1/2})}^{2},

for some constant
\lambda>0
. If we can prove

(16)

(Lf,f)_{L^{2}_{k}}\leq-C\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k}}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

then define
A=M\chi_{R},B=L-A
, where
\chi\in D({\mathbb{R}})
a truncation function which satisfies
\mathbb{1}_{[-1,1]}\leq\chi\leq\mathbb{1}_{[-2,2]}
and we denote
\chi_{a}(\cdot):=\chi(\cdot/a)
for some constant
a>0
. Taking
M,R
large we have

(Bf,f)_{L^{2}_{k}}\leq-C\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k}}^{2},\quad\|Af\|_{L^{2}(\mu^{-1/2})}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k}},

which implies

\|S_{B}(t)f\|_{L^{2}_{k}}\leq e^{-\lambda t}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{k}},

by Duhamel’s formula

\|S_{L}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{k}\to L^{2}_{k}}\leq\|S_{B}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{k}\to L^{2}_{k}}+\int_{0}^{t}\|S_{L}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mu^{-1/2})\to L^{2}(\mu^{-1/2})}\|A\|_{L^{2}_{k}\to L^{2}(\mu^{-1/2})}\|S_{B}(t-s)\|_{L^{2}_{k}\to L^{2}_{k}}\leq Ce^{-\lambda t}.

The rate of convergence for the linear operator
L
is established. Define a scalar product by

((f,g))=(f,g)_{L^{2}_{k}}+\eta\int_{0}^{+\infty}(S_{L}(\tau)f,S_{L}(\tau)g)d\tau,

since

\int_{0}^{+\infty}(S_{L}(\tau)f,S_{L}(\tau)f)d\tau\leq C\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k}}^{2}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-2\lambda\tau}d\tau\leq C\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k}}^{2},

we deduce
((\cdot,\cdot))
is an equivalent norm to
L^{2}_{k}
. By

\int_{0}^{+\infty}(S_{L}(\tau)Lf,S_{L}(\tau)f)d\tau=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{d}{d\tau}\|S_{L}(\tau)f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}d\tau=\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\|S_{L}(\tau)f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=-\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

which implies

((Lf,f))=(Lf,f)_{L^{2}_{k}}+\eta\int_{0}^{\infty}(S_{L}(\tau)Lf,S_{L}(\tau)f)d\tau=-C_{1}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k}}+(C_{2}-\eta)\|f\|_{L^{2}}\leq-C_{1}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k}},

by choosing a suitable
\eta
. The estimate for the linearized operator
L
in this equivalent norm allows us to combine with the nonlinear estimates to conclude the full convergence. In other words, one of the main works of this paper is to prove (
16
) plus some appropriate upper bounds for the nonlinear operator which consists with the lower bound in the linearized estimate.

We briefly describe one proof for (
16
). We observed and proved, for any
\gamma\in(-3,1],k>\max\{\gamma+3,3\}

(17)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}|v_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}}dv_{*}d\sigma\leq\frac{c}{k^{\frac{\gamma+3}{4}}}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}+C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-2},\quad\forall v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3},

where the constant
c
is independent of
k
. The proof of (
17
) can be seen in Lemma
6.2
. By (
17
), under the cutoff assumption (A2), for the
Q^{+}
term we have

\displaystyle|(Q^{+}(\mu,f),f\langle v\rangle^{2k})|\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f(v^{\prime})|e^{-\frac{1}{2}\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{2}}|f(v)|\langle v\rangle^{2k}dv_{*}dvd\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f(v^{\prime})|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2k}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{2}}dv_{*}dvd\sigma\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{2k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2k}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{2}}|f(v)|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dv_{*}dvd\sigma\right)^{1/2}

(18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{2}}{k^{\frac{\gamma+3}{4}}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1}}^{2},

for some constant
c_{2}>0
independent of
k
, the
(Q^{+}(f,\mu),f\langle v\rangle^{2k})
term can be estimated by the same way. For the
Q^{-}
term we easily compute

-Q^{-}(f,\mu)-Q^{-}(\mu,f)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)(\mu(v_{*})f(v)+\mu(v)f(v_{*}))dv_{*}d\sigma\leq-c_{1}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}f(v)+C\mu(v)\|f\|_{L^{1}_{4}},

so we have

-(Q^{-}(f,\mu)+Q^{-}(\mu,f),f\langle v\rangle^{2k})\leq-c_{1}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{6}}^{2}.

Gathering the two terms we have

(Lf,f\langle v\rangle^{2k})\leq-(c_{1}-\frac{2c_{2}}{k^{\frac{\gamma+3}{4}}})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1}}^{2}.

If
k
is larger than some constant
k_{0}
such that
2c_{2}<c_{1}{k_{0}^{\frac{\gamma+3}{4}}}
, (
16
) is thus proved. To get an optimal
k
for the polynomial weight case, we will use the pre-post collisional change of variables to give more precise computation in this paper.

We remark here that (
17
) also serves an important point in the proof for Boltzmann equation with large amplitude initial data. In
[
16
]
, the following inequality

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{4}|v|^{2}}}{e^{-\frac{1}{4}|v^{\prime}|^{2}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}|v_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}}dv_{*}d\sigma\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-2},

plays a key role in the proof for Boltzmann equation with large amplitude initial data for the
\mu^{-1/2}
case. The inequality (
17
) can be seen as its polynomial version.

For the upper bound, we observed and proved such fact that

(19)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}dv_{*}d\sigma\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma},\quad\forall v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3},

which plays an essential role in the proof of the upper bound. Compared to the
\mu^{-1/2}
case, if we replace
\langle v\rangle^{k}
by
\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}=e^{\frac{1}{4}|v|^{2}}
in (
19
), we easily seen that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\frac{e^{\frac{1}{4}|v|^{2}}}{e^{\frac{1}{4}|v^{\prime}|^{2}}e^{\frac{1}{4}|v_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}}}dv_{*}d\sigma=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}e^{-\frac{1}{4}|v_{*}|^{2}}dv_{*}d\sigma\leq C\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}.

Now we explain the strategy of the proof for the Boltzmann equation with large amplitude initial data. For the polynomial weight case, let
f=\langle v\rangle^{-k}(F-\mu)
in (
1
), then
f
satisfies

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=L_{k}f+\Gamma_{k}(f,f),

with

L_{k}f=\langle v\rangle^{k}Q(f\langle v\rangle^{-k},\mu)+\langle v\rangle^{k}Q(\mu,f\langle v\rangle^{-k}),\quad\Gamma_{k}(f,f)=\langle v\rangle^{k}Q(f\langle v\rangle^{-k},f\langle v\rangle^{-k}).

For any
k\geq\max\{3,3+\gamma\}
, like
[
16
]
, instead of estimate the nonlinear term in the following way

|\langle v\rangle^{\beta}\Gamma_{k}(f,f)|\leq C\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}\|\langle v\rangle^{\beta}f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2},

we prove a new estimate

|\langle v\rangle^{\beta}\Gamma_{k}(f,f)|\leq C\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}\|\langle v\rangle^{\beta}f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2-a}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f(t,x,v)|dv\right)^{a},

for some constant
0<a<1
. Second, we observe that under the condition

H(F_{0})+\|\langle v\rangle^{k}f_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L_{v}^{\infty}}\leq\epsilon_{0},

we can prove that
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f(t,x,v)|dv
will be small after some positive time even if it could be initially large. This observation is the key point to control the nonlinear term
\Gamma_{k}(f,f)
, we can finally obtain the uniform
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
estimate under the smallness of
\|\langle v\rangle^{k}f_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{\infty}_{v}}
and
H(F_{0})
so that initial data is allowed to have large amplitude oscillations.

1.6.
Remark on the Cutoff assumption (A2)

Compared to the cutoff assumption (A2), in many text people define the cutoff assumption in the following way:

(20)

K\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)d\theta\leq K^{-1}

which is weaker than assumption (A2). But assumption (A2) plays an important in our proof, for example, if we want to use (
17
) to prove (
1.5
), the assumption (
20
) is not enough, we need to assume that (A2) holds.

In the proof of Theorem
1.2
, we use the prepost-collisional change of variable instead of using inequalities like (
17
), thus under assumption (
20
), Theorem
1.2
is still true, actually Theorem
1.2
can be proved for the Boltzmann equation without cutoff, see
[
9
]
.

But for Theorem
1.3
we need to assume that (A2) holds since technically we need to use inequality like (
17
) to prove it.

1.7.
Organization of the paper

Technical tools and lemmas are listed in Section
2
. Section
3
is devoted to the upper bounds and coercivity estimate on collision operator
Q
. In Section
4
we will prove estimates for the inhomogeneous equation. We obtain global well-posedness and rate of convergence in
L^{2}
in Section
5
. Section
6
and Section
7
are devoted to the proof for the Boltzmann equation with large amplitude initial data. In Section
6
we prove global well-posedness for the Boltzmann equation with large amplitude initial data and in Section
7
we establish rate of convergence to the equilibrium.

2.
Preliminaries

In later analysis, we often use two types of change of variables below.

Lemma 2.1
.

(
[
1
]
) For any smooth function
f
we have

(1) (Regular change of variables)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f(v^{\prime})d\sigma dv=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\frac{1}{\cos^{3+\gamma}(\theta/2)}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f(v)d\sigma dv.

(2) (Singular change of variables)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f(v^{\prime})d\sigma dv_{*}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\frac{1}{\sin^{3+\gamma}(\theta/2)}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f(v_{*})d\sigma dv_{*}.

Lemma 2.2
.

For any smooth function
f,g,h,b
, for any constant
\gamma\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f_{*}gh^{\prime}dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\right)^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f_{*}|^{2}|g|dvdv_{*}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v^{\prime}|^{\gamma}|g||h^{\prime}|^{2}dvdv^{\prime}.

Similarly

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f_{*}gh^{\prime}dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\cos^{-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\right)^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f_{*}||g|^{2}dvdv_{*}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v_{*}-v^{\prime}|^{\gamma}|f_{*}||h^{\prime}|^{2}dv_{*}dv^{\prime}.

Proof.

For the first inequality, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and singular change of variables

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f_{*}gh^{\prime}dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f_{*}|^{2}|g|dvdv_{*}d\sigma\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}||g||h^{\prime}|^{2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\right)^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f_{*}|^{2}|g|dvdv_{*}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v^{\prime}|^{\gamma}|g||h^{\prime}|^{2}dvdv^{\prime},

which finishes the proof. The second inequality can be proved similarly by regular change of variables.
∎

Lemma 2.3
.

(
[
49
]
, Section 1.4)(Pre-post collisional change of variables)
For smooth function
F
we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}F(v,v_{*},v^{\prime},v_{*}^{\prime})B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos\theta)dvdv_{*}d\sigma=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}F(v^{\prime},v_{*}^{\prime},v,v_{*})B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos\theta)dvdv_{*}d\sigma.

Lemma 2.4
.

(Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality) (
[
39
]
, Chapter 4)
Let
1<p,r<+\infty
and
0<\lambda<d
with
1/p+\lambda/d+1/r=2
. Then there exists a constant
C(n,\lambda,p)
, such that for all smooth function
f
,
h
we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}f(x)|x-y|^{-\lambda}h(y)dxdy\leq C(n,\lambda,p)\|f\|_{L^{p}}\|h\|_{L^{r}}.

The following can be seen as a weak version of Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality when
q=+\infty
which is useful in the following proof.

Lemma 2.5
.

Suppose
\gamma\in(-3,0)
, then for any smooth function
f
, the following estimate holds:

If
\gamma\in(-\frac{3}{2},0)
, we have

\sup_{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|(v_{*})dv_{*}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{1+\frac{2\gamma}{3}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{-\frac{2\gamma}{3}}.

If
\gamma\in(-2,0)
, we have

\sup_{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|(v_{*})dv_{*}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{1+\frac{\gamma}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{3}}^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}.

If
\gamma\in(-3,0)
, we have

\sup_{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|(v_{*})dv_{*}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{1+\frac{\gamma}{3}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{-\frac{\gamma}{3}}.

Proof.

Assume
f
does not equal to
0
otherwise the estimate is trivial. Let
\lambda>0
be a constant to be determined. We divide the integral into two regions
|v-v_{*}|\leq\lambda
and
|v-v_{*}|>\lambda
we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|(v_{*})dv_{*}\leq\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\lambda}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|(v_{*})dv_{*}+\int_{|v-v_{*}|>\lambda}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|(v_{*})dv_{*}.

The second part is bounded by

\int_{|v-v_{*}|>\lambda}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|(v_{*})dv_{*}\lesssim\lambda^{\gamma}\|f\|_{L^{1}}.

For the first part we divided it into three cases, for
\gamma\in(-\frac{3}{2},0)
, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we have

\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\lambda}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|(v_{*})dv_{*}\leq\left(\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\lambda}|v-v_{*}|^{2\gamma}dv_{*}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}+\gamma}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

For
\gamma\in(-2,0)
, by Hölder’s inequality we have

\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\lambda}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|(v_{*})dv_{*}\leq\left(\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\lambda}|v-v_{*}|^{\frac{3}{2}\gamma}dv_{*}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\|f\|_{L^{3}}\lesssim\lambda^{2+\gamma}\|f\|_{L^{3}}.

For
\gamma\in(-3,0)
, we have

\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\lambda}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|(v_{*})dv_{*}\leq\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\lambda}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}dv_{*}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\lambda^{3+\gamma}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}},

so the proof is ended by taking
\lambda=\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{2}{3}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{-\frac{2}{3}},\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{3}}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{3}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{-\frac{1}{3}}
respectively.
∎

Lemma 2.6
.

For any smooth function
f,g
, for any
\gamma\in(-2,0)
we have

\mathcal{R}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f(v_{*})|^{2}|g(v)|^{2}dvdv_{*}\lesssim\min_{m+n=1}\{\|f\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{H^{n}_{2}}^{2},\|f\|_{H^{m}_{2}}^{2}\|g\|_{H^{n}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\}.

Similarly For any
\gamma\in(-3,-2]
we have

\mathcal{R}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f(v_{*})|^{2}|g(v)|^{2}dvdv_{*}\lesssim\min_{m+n=2}\{\|f\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{H^{n}_{2}}^{2},\|f\|_{H^{m}_{2}}^{2}\|g\|_{H^{n}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we only prove that

\mathcal{R}\lesssim\min_{m+n=1}\{\|f\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{H^{n}_{2}}^{2}\},\quad\gamma\in(-2,0),\quad\mathcal{R}\lesssim\min_{m+n=2}\{\|f\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{H^{n}_{2}}^{2}\},\quad\gamma\in(-3,-2].

For both
\gamma\in(-2,0)
and
\gamma\in(-3,-2]
, by

C\langle v_{*}\rangle^{-\gamma}\leq\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{-\gamma}\langle v\rangle^{-\gamma},

we compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{-\gamma}|f(v_{*})|^{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}|g(v)|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{-\gamma}dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma})|f(v_{*})|^{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}|g(v)|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{-\gamma}dvdv_{*}:=I_{1}+I_{2}.

For the
I_{1}
term, for both
\gamma\in(-2,0)
and
\gamma\in(-3,-2]
we easily compute

I_{1}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{-\gamma/2}}^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{2}}^{2}.

For the
I_{2}
term, when
\gamma\in(-2,0)
by Lemma
2.5
we have

\displaystyle I_{2}\lesssim\left(\sup_{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f(v_{*})|^{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}dv_{*}\right)\|g\|_{L^{2}_{-\gamma/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f^{2}\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}}^{1+\frac{\gamma}{2}}\|f^{2}\|_{L^{3}_{\gamma}}^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2+\gamma}\|f\|_{L^{6}_{\gamma/2}}^{-\gamma}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{1}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{2}}^{2},

similarly

\displaystyle I_{2}\lesssim\left(\sup_{v_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|g(v)|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{-\gamma}dv\right)\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{H^{1}_{2}}^{2},

the case
\gamma\in(-2,0)
is thus proved. When
\gamma\in(-3,-2]
, by Lemma
2.5
we have

\displaystyle I_{2}\lesssim\left(\sup_{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f(v_{*})|^{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{\gamma}dv_{*}\right)\|g\|_{L^{2}_{-\gamma/2}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f^{2}\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}}^{1+\frac{\gamma}{3}}\|f^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\gamma}}^{-\frac{\gamma}{3}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2+\frac{2\gamma}{3}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\gamma/2}}^{-\frac{2\gamma}{3}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{2}}^{2},

similarly

I_{2}\lesssim\left(\sup_{v_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|g(v)|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{-\gamma}dv\right)\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{H^{2}_{2}}^{2},

and by Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality

I_{2}\lesssim\|f^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{\gamma}}\|g^{2}\|_{L^{3}_{-\gamma}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2p}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{L^{6}_{-\gamma/2}}^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{1}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}\|g\|_{H^{1}_{2}}^{2},

where
p=\frac{3}{5+\gamma}
implies
2p\in[2,3)
. So the case
\gamma\in(-3,-2]
is proved by combining the three cases.
∎

We will use the following representation of
v^{\prime}
which can be proved directly. We have

(21)

\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2}=\langle v\rangle^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|v\cdot\omega,\quad\omega\perp(v-v_{*}),\quad v\cdot w=v_{*}\cdot w,

where
\omega=\frac{\sigma-(\sigma\cdot k)k}{|\sigma-(\sigma\cdot k)k|}
with
k=\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}
. We have the following estimate for the term
\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}
.

Lemma 2.7
.

For any constant
k\geq 4
we have

(22)

\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}=\sin^{k}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k}+R_{1}+R_{2},\quad|R_{1}|\leq C_{k}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k-1}\langle v\rangle,\quad|R_{2}|\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{k},

for some constant
C_{k}>0
. We also have

(23)

\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}-\langle v\rangle^{k}\cos^{k}\frac{\theta}{2}=k\langle v\rangle^{k-2}\cos^{k-1}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|(v\cdot\omega)+L_{1}+L_{2},

with

|L_{1}|\leq C_{k}\sin^{k-2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k}\langle v\rangle^{2},\quad|L_{2}|\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{k-2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

for some constant
C_{k}>0
, in particular

\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}=\cos^{k}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v\rangle^{k}+Q_{1},\quad|Q_{1}|\leq C_{k}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k}\langle v\rangle^{k-1}.

Proof.

For simplicity, we prove the result for
\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2k}
. By (
21
) and mean value theorem we have

\displaystyle\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2k}-\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2k}\sin^{2k}\frac{\theta}{2}=

\displaystyle k\int_{0}^{1}\left(\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+t(\langle v\rangle^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|v\cdot\omega)\right)^{k-1}dt

\displaystyle\times\left(\langle v\rangle^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|v\cdot\omega\right),

since
v\cdot w=v_{*}\cdot w
which implies
|v-v_{*}||v\cdot\omega|\leq|v||v_{*}\cdot\omega|+|v_{*}||v\cdot\omega|\leq 2|v||v_{*}|
, so we have

\langle v\rangle^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|v\cdot\omega\leq\langle v\rangle^{2}+4\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v||v_{*}|\leq 3\langle v\rangle^{2}+2\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2},

so if
2k-1\geq 2
we have

\displaystyle\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2k}-\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2k}\sin^{2k}\frac{\theta}{2}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}+\langle v\rangle^{2})^{k-1}(\langle v\rangle^{2}+\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v_{*}||v|)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(\sin^{2k-2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2k-2}+\langle v\rangle^{2k-2})(\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle+\langle v\rangle)\langle v\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2k-1}\langle v\rangle+C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{2k},

(
22
) is thus proved. For (
23
), using (
21
) and the mean value theorem twice we have

\displaystyle\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2k}-\langle v\rangle^{2k}\cos^{2k}\frac{\theta}{2}-2k(\langle v\rangle^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})^{k-1}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|(v\cdot\omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k(\langle v\rangle^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})^{k-1}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle+k(k-1)\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\left(\langle v\rangle^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+t(\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|v\cdot\omega)\right)^{k-2}dt

\displaystyle\times\left(\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|v\cdot\omega\right)^{2}:=I_{1}+I_{2}.

For
I_{1}
we easily deduce

I_{1}\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{2k-2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

since
|v-v_{*}||v\cdot\omega|\leq 2|v||v_{*}|
, we have

\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|v\cdot\omega\leq\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+4|v||v_{*}|\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\leq 3\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\langle v\rangle^{2},

so we have

\displaystyle I_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+4|v||v_{*}|\sin\frac{\theta}{2})^{2}(3\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+3\langle v\rangle^{2})^{k-2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}(\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}+\langle v\rangle^{2})(\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2k-4}\sin^{2k-4}\frac{\theta}{2}+\langle v\rangle^{2k-4})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}\langle v\rangle^{2}(\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2k-4}\sin^{2k-4}\frac{\theta}{2}+\langle v\rangle^{2k-4})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\sin^{2k-2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2k}\langle v\rangle^{2}+C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{2k-2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

if
2k\geq 4
, so the theorem is thus proved.
∎

Lemma 2.8
.

For any smooth function
g
and
f
, if
\gamma>-3
we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}g_{*}fdv_{*}dv\leq C\|g\|_{L^{2}_{l}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l}}^{2},

with
l=\max\{\gamma+2,\frac{3}{2}\}
.

Proof.

We split it into two cases
\gamma\geq 0
and
\gamma<0
, for the case
\gamma\geq 0
, we easily compute

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}g_{*}fdv_{*}dv\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}}\|g\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma+2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma+2}}.

For the case
\gamma<0
, by Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}g_{*}fdv_{*}dv\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}}\|g\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{3/2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{3/2}},

where
\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{2}(2+\frac{\gamma}{3})\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
implies
p\in(1,2)
. The proof is thus finished by gathering the two cases.
∎

Lemma 2.9
.

For any
\gamma>-3
,
k>\max\{3,3+\gamma\}
we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{-k}dv_{*}\leq\frac{c}{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}+C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-2}.

Proof.

For the case
\gamma>0
, we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{-k}dv_{*}\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(|v|^{\gamma}+|v_{*}|^{\gamma})\langle v_{*}\rangle^{-k}dv_{*},

It is easily seen that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{-k}dv_{*}\leq C_{k}\leq\frac{c}{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}+C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-2},

recall the theory of beta function (
5
) we have

\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{-k}dv_{*}\leq C\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{+\infty}r^{2}\frac{1}{(1+r^{2})^{k/2}}dr\leq\frac{c}{k^{3/2}}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma},

so the case
\gamma\in[0,1]
is thus proved. For the case
\gamma\in(-3,0)
, If
|v|\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we have
|v_{*}|+\frac{1}{2}\leq 1+|v-v_{*}|
, so we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{-k}dv_{*}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{-k}dv_{*}\leq C_{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{-k}dv_{*}\leq C_{k}\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-2}.

Consider now
|v|>\frac{1}{2}
and split the integral into two regions
|v-v_{*}|>\langle v\rangle/4
and
|v-v_{*}|\leq\langle v\rangle/4
. For the first region we obtain

\int_{|v-v_{*}|>\frac{\langle v\rangle}{4}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{-k}dv_{*}\leq C\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{-k}dv_{*}\leq C\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}\frac{1}{(1+r^{2})^{k/2}}dr\leq\frac{c}{k^{3/2}}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}.

For the second region,
|v|>1/2
and
|v-v_{*}|\leq\langle v\rangle/4
imply
|v_{*}|\geq\langle v\rangle/8
, hence

\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\frac{\langle v\rangle}{4}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{-k}dv_{*}\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{-k}\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\frac{\langle v\rangle}{4}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}dv_{*}\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{-k+\gamma+3}\leq\frac{c}{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}+C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-2},

so the theorem is thus proved.
∎

We introduce a change of variable which will be used frequently.

Lemma 2.10
.

(
[
44
]
, Lemma A.1.)
For any non negative function
F
in terms of
v_{*},v,r=|v-v_{*}|,\theta,v^{\prime},v_{*}^{\prime}
, we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}Fd\sigma dv_{*}=4\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|}\int_{\{w:w\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}F\frac{1}{r}dwdv^{\prime},

where
r,\theta,v_{*}^{\prime},v_{*}
in the left hand side is changed to

r=\sqrt{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}+|w|^{2}},\quad\cos(\theta/2)=|w|/r,\quad v_{*}^{\prime}=v+w,\quad v_{*}=v^{\prime}+w,

respectively.

Lemma 2.11
.

For any
-3<\gamma\leq 2
, for any constant
k>\max\{3,3+\gamma\}
we have

I:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}dv_{*}d\sigma\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma},

for all
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. In fact for
\gamma\in[0,1]
we can prove a stronger estimate

I:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}dv_{*}d\sigma\leq\frac{c}{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}+C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-2}.

Proof.

If
|v|\leq 1
, since
\langle v_{*}\rangle\leq\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle\langle v^{\prime}\rangle
, we have

I\leq 2^{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\frac{1}{\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k}}dv_{*}d\sigma\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-2},

we focus on the case
|v|\geq 1
later. We first prove the case
\gamma\in[0,1]
. By Lemma
2.10
and since
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}\int_{\{w:w\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}+|w|^{2}}}(\sqrt{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}+|w|^{2}})^{\gamma}\langle v+w\rangle^{-k}dwdv^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2-\gamma}}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}\int_{\{w:w\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}\langle v+w\rangle^{-k}dwdv^{\prime}.

We first split
v
into two parts

(24)

v=v_{\perp}+v_{\parallel},\quad v_{\perp}=\frac{v\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)}{|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}}(v^{\prime}-v),\quad|v|^{2}=|v_{\perp}|^{2}+|v_{\parallel}|^{2},\quad v_{\perp}\perp v_{\parallel},\quad v_{\parallel}\parallel w,\quad|v+w|^{2}=|w+v_{\parallel}|^{2}+|v_{\perp}|^{2}.

Then the integral becomes

\int_{\{w:w\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}\langle v+w\rangle^{-k}dw=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+|w+v_{\parallel}|^{2}+|v_{\perp}|^{2})^{k/2}}dw=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+|w|^{2}+|v_{\perp}|^{2})^{k/2}}dw,

making a change of variable
w=\sqrt{1+|v_{\perp}|^{2}}x
we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+|w|^{2}+|v_{\perp}|^{2})^{k/2}}dw=(1+|v_{\perp}|^{2})^{1-\frac{k}{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+|x|^{2})^{k/2}}dx=2\pi\frac{1}{k-2}(1+|v_{\perp}|^{2})^{1-\frac{k}{2}},

so
I
turns to

I\leq 2\pi\frac{1}{k-2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2-\gamma}}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}\frac{1}{\langle v_{\perp}\rangle^{k-2}}dv^{\prime},\quad|v_{\perp}|=\frac{|v\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)|}{|v-v^{\prime}|}.

Since
|v_{\perp}|\leq|v|
, so we split it into three cases
|v_{\perp}|\geq(1-\frac{1}{k})|v|
,
|v_{\perp}|\leq\frac{|v|}{k}
and
\frac{1}{k}|v|\leq|v_{\perp}|\leq(1-\frac{1}{k})|v|
. For the case
|v_{\perp}|\geq(1-\frac{1}{k})|v|
we have

\langle v\rangle^{k-2}\leq(1+\frac{1}{k-1})^{k-2}\langle v_{\perp}\rangle^{k-2}\leq e\langle v_{\perp}\rangle^{k-2},

together with Lemma
2.9
we have

I\leq\frac{c}{k}\langle v\rangle^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2-\gamma}}\frac{1}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}dv^{\prime}\leq\frac{c}{k^{2}}\langle v\rangle^{2}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-2}+C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{2}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-4}\leq\frac{c}{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}+C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-2}.

For the case
|v_{\perp}|\leq\frac{1}{k}|v|
we have

|v^{\prime}|^{2}=|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v|^{2}+2v\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)\geq|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v|^{2}-2|v-v^{\prime}||v_{\perp}|\geq(1-\frac{1}{k})(|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v|^{2}),

which implies

\displaystyle I\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2-\gamma}}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{(1+|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v|^{2})^{k/2}}\frac{1}{\langle v_{\perp}\rangle^{k-2}}dv^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2-\gamma}}\frac{1}{(1+\frac{|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}}{1+|v|^{2}})^{k/2}}\frac{1}{\langle v_{\perp}\rangle^{k-2}}dv^{\prime}.

We make the change of variables

(25)

r=|v-v^{\prime}|,\quad v\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=r|v|\cos\theta,\quad dv^{\prime}=r^{2}\sin\theta drd\theta d\phi,\quad|v_{\perp}|^{2}=|v|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta,\quad|v_{\parallel}|^{2}=|v|^{2}\sin^{2}\theta.

So
I
turns to

\displaystyle I\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{k}2\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{\gamma}\frac{1}{(1+\frac{r^{2}}{1+|v|^{2}})^{k/2}}\int_{0}^{\pi}\frac{1}{\langle|v|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\rangle^{k-2}}\sin\theta drd\theta.

Making another change of variables

(26)

r=\sqrt{1+|v|^{2}}x,\quad y=|v|\cos\theta,\quad dy=|v|\sin\theta d\theta,

then by (
5
) we deduce

\displaystyle I\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{k}2\pi\frac{1}{|v|}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma+1}\int_{0}^{\infty}x^{\gamma}\frac{1}{(1+|x|^{2})^{k/2}}\int_{-|v|}^{|v|}\frac{1}{\langle y\rangle^{k-2}}dydx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{k}2\pi\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{\infty}x^{\gamma}\frac{1}{(1+|x|^{2})^{k/2}}dx\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\langle y\rangle^{k-2}}dy\leq\frac{c}{k}2\pi\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}.

For the case
\frac{1}{k}|v|\leq|v_{\perp}|\leq(1-\frac{1}{k})|v|
we have

\langle v\rangle^{k-2}\leq k^{k-2}\langle v_{\perp}\rangle^{k-2}\leq C_{k}\langle v_{\perp}\rangle^{k-2},\quad|v^{\prime}|^{2}\geq|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v|^{2}-2|v-v^{\prime}||v_{\perp}|\geq\frac{1}{k}(|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v|^{2}),

still make the same change of variables as (
25
) and (
26
) we have

\displaystyle I\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2-\gamma}}\frac{1}{(1+|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v|^{2})^{k/2}}dv^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{2-k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2-\gamma}}\frac{1}{(1+\frac{|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}}{1+|v|^{2}})^{k/2}}dv^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}2\pi\langle v\rangle^{2-k}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{\gamma}\frac{1}{(1+\frac{r^{2}}{1+|v|^{2}})^{k/2}}drd\theta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}2\pi\langle v\rangle^{2+\gamma-k}\int_{0}^{\infty}x^{\gamma}\frac{1}{(1+x^{2})^{k/2}}dx\leq\frac{c}{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}+C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-2},

the case
\gamma\in[0,1]
is thus proved. For the case
\gamma\in(-3,0)
, since
\langle v\rangle^{k}\leq C_{k}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}+C_{k}\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{k}
, we have

I\leq C_{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\frac{1}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}dv_{*}d\sigma+C_{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\frac{1}{\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}dv_{*}d\sigma:=I_{1}+I_{2}.

We only prove the term
I_{1}
since the
I_{2}
term is easily achieved by interchange
v^{\prime}
and
v_{*}^{\prime}
. For the
I_{1}
term by Lemma
2.10
we have

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|}\frac{1}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}\int_{\{w:w\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}+|w|^{2}}}(\sqrt{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}+|w|^{2}})^{\gamma}dwdv^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|}\frac{1}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\frac{1}{(|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}+|w|^{2})^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}}dwdv^{\prime},

by a change of variable
w=|v-v^{\prime}|x
we deduce

I_{1}\leq C_{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v^{\prime}-v|^{\gamma}\frac{1}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+|x|^{2})^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}}dxdv^{\prime}\leq C_{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v^{\prime}-v|^{\gamma}\frac{1}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}dv^{\prime},\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma},

since
\gamma<0
implies
1-\gamma>1
so
(1+|x|^{2})^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}
is integrable, the case
\gamma\in(-3,0)
is thus proved. For the case
\gamma\in[1,2]
, since
1\leq\gamma
, we have

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}\int_{\{w:w\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}+|w|^{2}}}(\sqrt{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}+|w|^{2}})^{\gamma}\langle v+w\rangle^{-k}dwdv^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2-\gamma}}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}\int_{\{w:w\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}\langle v+w\rangle^{-k}dwdv^{\prime}

\displaystyle+C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}\int_{\{w:w\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}|w|^{\gamma-1}\langle v+w\rangle^{-k}dwdv^{\prime}:=I_{1}+I_{2},

since
\gamma\leq 2
, the
I_{1}
term is the same as the term
I
in the case
\gamma\in[0,1]
so can be estimated by the same way. We now focus on the
I_{2}
term, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\{w:w\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}|w|^{\gamma-1}\langle v+w\rangle^{-k}dw=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\frac{|w|^{\gamma-1}}{(1+|w+v_{\parallel}|^{2}+|v_{\perp}|^{2})^{k/2}}dw

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\frac{|w|^{\gamma-1}+|v_{\parallel}|^{\gamma-1}}{(1+|w|^{2}+|v_{\perp}|^{2})^{k/2}}dw.

Making the change of variable
|w|=\sqrt{1+|v_{\perp}|^{2}}x
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\frac{|w|^{\gamma-1}+|v_{\parallel}|^{\gamma-1}}{(1+|w|^{2}+|v_{\perp}|^{2})^{k/2}}dw\leq

\displaystyle|v_{\parallel}|^{\gamma-1}(1+|v_{\perp}|^{2})^{1-\frac{k}{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+|x|^{2})^{k/2}}dx

\displaystyle+\langle v_{\perp}\rangle^{\gamma-1}(1+|v_{\perp}|^{2})^{1-\frac{k}{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\frac{|x|^{\gamma}}{(1+|x|^{2})^{k/2}}dx

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-1}(1+|v_{\perp}|^{2})^{1-\frac{k}{2}},

we decude

I_{2}\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}\frac{1}{\langle v_{\perp}\rangle^{k-2}}dv^{\prime},

it is easily seen that
I_{2}
also be estimated by the same way as the term
I
in the case
\gamma\in[0,1]
, so the proof is thus finished.
∎

For the exponential weight case, we have a better estimate for the linearized operator.

Lemma 2.12
.

For any
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, for any constant
a>0
and
b\in(0,2)
we have

I:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\frac{e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}}}{e^{a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}|v_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}}dv_{*}d\sigma\leq C_{a,b}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-\frac{b(\gamma+3)}{4}},

for all
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.

Proof.

Since
\gamma-1\leq 0
, by Lemma
2.10
we have

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=4\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|}\frac{e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}}}{e^{a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}}\int_{\{\omega:\omega\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}+|w|^{2}}}(\sqrt{|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}+|w|^{2}})^{\gamma}e^{-\frac{|v+w|^{2}}{2}}dwdv^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 4\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}}\frac{e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}}}{e^{a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}}\int_{\{\omega:\omega\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}|w|^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}e^{-\frac{|v+w|^{2}}{2}}dwdv^{\prime}.

Recall the decomposition (
24
) we have

I\leq 4\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{|v^{\prime}-v|^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}}\frac{e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}}}{e^{a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}}e^{-\frac{|v_{\perp}|^{2}}{2}}\int_{\{\omega:\omega\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}|w|^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}e^{-\frac{|v_{\parallel}+w|^{2}}{2}}dwdv^{\prime}.

Since
\frac{\gamma-1}{2}>-2
, we have

\int_{\{\omega:\omega\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)=0\}}|w|^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}e^{-\frac{|v_{\parallel}+w|^{2}}{2}}dw=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}|w-v_{\parallel}|^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}e^{-\frac{|w|^{2}}{2}}dw\leq C\langle v_{\parallel}\rangle^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}},

which implies

I\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v^{\prime}-v|^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}\frac{e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}}}{e^{a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}}e^{-\frac{|v_{\perp}|^{2}}{2}}\langle v_{\parallel}\rangle^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}dv^{\prime}.

If
|v|\leq 1
, since
\frac{\gamma-3}{2}>-3
it is easily seen that

I\leq C_{a,b}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v^{\prime}-v|^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}\frac{1}{e^{a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}}dv^{\prime}\leq C_{a,b}\leq C_{a,b}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-\frac{b(\gamma+3)}{4}},

so we focus on the
|v|>1
case. We split it into two case
|v_{\perp}|\geq\frac{|v|}{2}
and
|v_{\perp}|<\frac{|v|}{2}
. For the case
|v_{\perp}|\geq\frac{|v|}{2}
we easily compute

I\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v^{\prime}-v|^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}\frac{e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}}}{e^{a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{8}}dv^{\prime}\leq C_{a,b}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{16}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v^{\prime}-v|^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}\frac{1}{e^{a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}}dv^{\prime}\leq C_{a,b}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma-b}.

For the case
|v_{\perp}|<\frac{|v|}{2}
we have
|v_{\parallel}|\geq\frac{|v|}{2}
which implies
\langle v_{\parallel}\rangle^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\leq C\langle v\rangle^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}
, so we have

I\leq C\langle v\rangle^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v^{\prime}-v|^{\frac{\gamma-3}{2}}\frac{e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}}}{e^{a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}}e^{-\frac{|v_{\perp}|^{2}}{2}}dv^{\prime}.

Still take the change of variables (
25
), since
|v^{\prime}|^{2}=|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v|^{2}+2v\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)
,
I
turns to

I\leq C\langle v\rangle^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{\pi}r^{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}e^{a(1+|v|^{2})^{\frac{b}{2}}-a(1+|v|^{2}+r^{2}+2r|v|\cos\theta)^{\frac{b}{2}}}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{2}}\sin\theta drd\theta.

Since
0<b<2
, there exists a constant
C_{a,b}
such that

\frac{|x|^{2}}{4}+C_{a,b}>4a\langle x\rangle^{b},\quad\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}},\quad x^{\frac{b}{2}}+y^{\frac{b}{2}}\geq(x+y)^{\frac{b}{2}},\quad\forall x,y\geq 0,

which implies that

\frac{|v|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{4}+C_{a,b}\geq a(4+4|v|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)^{\frac{b}{2}},

and

a(1+|v|^{2}+r^{2}+2r|v|\cos\theta)^{\frac{b}{2}}+a(4+4|v|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)^{\frac{b}{2}}\geq a(1+|v|^{2}+r^{2}+2r|v|\cos\theta+4|v|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)^{\frac{b}{2}}\geq a(1+|v|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}r^{2})^{\frac{b}{2}}.

So we have

I\leq C_{a,b}\langle v\rangle^{\frac{\gamma-1}{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{\pi}r^{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}e^{a(1+|v|^{2})^{\frac{b}{2}}-a(1+|v|^{2}+\frac{r^{2}}{2})^{\frac{b}{2}}}e^{-\frac{|v|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{4}}\sin\theta drd\theta.

As before, taking another change of variables (
26
) we have

\displaystyle I\leq

\displaystyle C_{a,b}\frac{1}{|v|}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma+1}\int_{0}^{+\infty}x^{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}e^{a(1+|v|^{2})^{\frac{b}{2}}-a(1+|v|^{2}+\frac{x^{2}(1+|v|^{2})}{2})^{\frac{b}{2}}}dx\int_{-|v|}^{|v|}e^{-\frac{y^{2}}{4}}dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{a,b}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{+\infty}x^{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}e^{a(1+|v|^{2})^{\frac{b}{2}}(1-(1+\frac{x^{2}}{2})^{\frac{b}{2}})}dx\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{y^{2}}{4}}dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{a,b}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{+\infty}x^{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}(1-(1+\frac{x^{2}}{2})^{\frac{b}{2}})}dx.

We split it into to two case
x\geq 2
and
x\in[0,2]
. For the case
x\geq 2
we have
(1+\frac{x^{2}}{2})^{\frac{b}{2}}-1\geq C_{b}\langle x\rangle^{b}
for some
C_{b}>0
. Together with
\langle v\rangle^{b}+\langle x\rangle^{b}\leq 2\langle x\rangle^{b}\langle v\rangle^{b}
implies

\displaystyle\int_{2}^{+\infty}x^{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}(1-(1+\frac{x^{2}}{2})^{\frac{b}{2}})}dx\leq

\displaystyle C_{b}\int_{2}^{+\infty}x^{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}e^{-aC_{b}\langle v\rangle^{b}\langle x\rangle^{b}}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{b}e^{-\frac{a}{2}C_{b}\langle v\rangle^{b}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}x^{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}e^{-\frac{a}{2}C_{b}\langle x\rangle^{b}}dx\leq C_{a,b}\langle v\rangle^{-b}.

For the case
x\in[0,2]
, we have
(1+\frac{x^{2}}{2})^{\frac{b}{2}}-1\geq C_{b}x^{2}
for some constant
C_{b}>0
. Making the change of variable
z=\langle v\rangle^{\frac{b}{2}}x
, since
\frac{\gamma+1}{2}>-1
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}x^{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}(1-(1+\frac{x^{2}}{2})^{\frac{b}{2}})}dx\leq

\displaystyle C_{b}\int_{0}^{+\infty}x^{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}e^{-aC_{b}\langle v\rangle^{b}x^{2}}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{b}\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{b(\gamma+3)}{4}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}z^{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}e^{-aC_{b}|z|^{b}}dz\leq C_{a,b}\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{b(\gamma+3)}{4}},

so the theorem is thus proved by gathering the terms together.
∎

We introduce the following lemma about relative entropy which will be used later.

Lemma 2.13
.

(
[
34
]
,
[
16
]
, Lemma 2.7) For any smooth function
F
satisfies (
2
) and (
3
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{|F(t,x,v)-\mu(v)|^{2}}{4\mu(v)}1_{|F(t,x,v-\mu(v)|\leq\mu(v)}dvdx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{1}{4}|F(t,x,v)-\mu(v)|1_{|F(t,x,v)-\mu(v)|\geq\mu(v)}dvdx\leq H(F_{0}),

where the relative entropy
H
is defined in (
14
).

The following estimate is established for the weight function
w(\alpha,\beta)
defined in (
10
).

Lemma 2.14
.

Suppose
|\alpha|,|\beta|
non-negative integers,
k\in{\mathbb{R}}
, the weight function
w(\alpha,\beta)
satisfies the following properties

w(|\alpha|,|\beta|)\leq w(|\alpha|,|\beta_{1}|),\quad w(|\alpha|,|\beta|)\leq w(|\alpha_{1}|,|\beta|),\quad\forall 0\leq|\alpha_{1}|<|\alpha|,\quad 0\leq|\beta_{1}|<|\beta|,

and

(27)

w(|\alpha|,|\beta|)\leq\langle v\rangle^{\min\{\gamma,0\}}w(|\alpha|+1,|\beta|-1),\quad\forall|\alpha|\geq 0,\quad\forall|\beta|\geq 1,

which implies

\langle v\rangle^{k}\leq\min_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 4}\{w(|\alpha|,|\beta|)\}.

We also have

(28)

\max_{|\alpha|+|\beta|=3}w^{2}(\alpha,\beta)\leq w(0,2)w(1,2),

and

(29)

\max_{|\alpha|+|\beta|=4}w^{2}(\alpha,\beta)\leq\min\{w(1,2)^{4/5}w(2,2)^{6/5},w(1,2)w(2,2),w(0,3)w(1,3)\}.

Proof.

The first is just form the fact that
a,b\geq 0
. (
27
) just follows form the fact that

k-a|\alpha|-b|\beta|+c\leq k-a(|\alpha|+1)-b(|\beta|-1)+c+\min\{\gamma,0\}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad b-a+\min\{\gamma,0\}\geq 0,

and we conclude form the definition of
a
and
b
. Then third statement is just by

\langle v\rangle^{k}=w(0,4)=\min_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 4}\{w(|\alpha|,|\beta|)\}.

For equation (
28
) we easily compute

\max_{|\alpha|+|\beta|=3}w^{2}(\alpha,\beta)=w^{2}(3,0)=2k-6a+2c\leq 2k-a-4b+2c=w(0,2)w(1,2).

For equation (
28
) and(
29
), since
7a=6b
, we easily compute

\max_{|\alpha|+|\beta|=4}w^{2}(\alpha,\beta)=w^{2}(4,0)=2k-8a+2c\leq 2k-\frac{16}{5}a-4b+2c=w(1,2)^{4/5}w(2,2)^{6/5},

and

\max_{|\alpha|+|\beta|=4}w^{2}(\alpha,\beta)=w^{2}(4,0)=2k-8a+2c=2k-6b-a+2c=w(0,3)w(1,3)\leq w(1,2)w(2,2),

so the proof of the lemma is thus finished.
∎

Next we prove a lemma related to the exponential weight case.

Lemma 2.15
.

For any
a>0,b\in(0,2),k\geq 0
, define
f(x):=a(1+x)^{\frac{b}{2}}-\frac{k}{2}\ln(1+x),x\geq 0,f(0)=a
, then the following two statements holds

f(c)\leq f(d)+C_{k,a,b},\quad\forall 0\leq c\leq d,\quad f(c+d)\leq f(c)+f(d)+C_{k,a,b},\quad\forall c,d\geq 0,

for some constant
C_{k,a,b}>0
independent of
c,d
.

Proof.

We easily compute that

f^{\prime}(x)=a\frac{b}{2}(1+x)^{\frac{b}{2}-1}-\frac{k}{2}\frac{1}{1+x},\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}},\quad x\geq 0,

we easily we have there exists a constant
e
which may depend on
k,a,b
such that

f^{\prime}(x)<0,\quad\hbox{if}\quad x\leq e,\quad f^{\prime}(x)\geq 0,\quad\hbox{if}\quad x\geq e,

so we have

\max_{0\leq c\leq d}f(c)-f(d)=f(0)-f(e)\leq C_{k,a,b},\quad\hbox{if}\quad e>0,\quad\max_{0\leq c\leq d}f(c)-f(d)=0,\quad\hbox{if}\quad e\leq 0,

so the first statement is thus proved. For the second statement since

f^{\prime\prime}(x)=a\frac{b}{2}(\frac{b}{2}-1)(1+x)^{b/2-2}+\frac{k}{2}\frac{1}{(1+x)^{2}},\quad x\geq 0,

Since
0<b/2<1
, there exists a constant
f
which may depend on
k,a,b
such that

f^{\prime\prime}(x)\geq 0,\quad\hbox{if}\quad x\leq f,\quad f^{\prime\prime}(x)<0,\quad\hbox{if}\quad x\geq f.

Thus for any
c,d\geq 0
, we easily compute that

f(c+d)-f(c)-f(d)+f(0)=\int_{0}^{d}\int_{0}^{c}f^{\prime\prime}(x+s)dsdx\leq\int_{0}^{\max\{f,0\}}\int_{0}^{\max\{f,0\}}|f^{\prime\prime}(x+s)|dsdx\leq C_{k,a,b},

so the second statement is thus proved since
f(0)=a
.
∎

Lemma 2.16
.

For any constant
k\in{\mathbb{R}},a>0,b\in(0,2)
we have

\frac{e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}}}{e^{a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}e^{a\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}}\leq C_{k,a,b}\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k}\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{k}},

for some constant
C_{k,a,b}>0
.

Proof.

The case
k\leq 0
is easy, since
b\in(0,2)
we easily have

\frac{e^{a\langle v\rangle^{b}}}{e^{a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}e^{a\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{b}}}\leq 1\leq\frac{\langle v\rangle^{k}}{\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k}\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{k}}.

We focus on the case
k\geq 0
later. This is equivalent to prove that

a\langle v\rangle^{b}-k\ln\langle v\rangle\leq a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}-k\ln\langle v^{\prime}\rangle+a\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{b}-k\ln\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle+C_{k,a,b},

for some constant
C_{k,a,b}
independent of
v
. By Lemma
2.15
we have

\displaystyle a\langle v\rangle^{b}-k\ln\langle v\rangle=f(|v|^{2})\leq

\displaystyle f(|v_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}+|v^{\prime}|^{2})+C_{k,a,b}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle f(|v_{*}^{\prime}|^{2})+f(|v^{\prime}|^{2})+C_{k,a,b}=a\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{b}-k\ln\langle v^{\prime}\rangle+a\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle^{b}-k\ln\langle v_{*}^{\prime}\rangle+C_{k,a,b},

where
f
is defined in Lemma
2.15
, so the proof is thus finished.
∎

We also recall some basic interpolation on
x
, the proof is elementary and thus omitted.

Lemma 2.17
.

For any non-negative integer
m,n
, for any function
f
, for any constant
k\in{\mathbb{R}}
we have

\|fg\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\min_{m+n=2}\{\|f\|_{H^{m}_{x}}\|g\|_{H^{n}_{x}}\},

and

(30)

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{8/5}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\leq\|f\langle v\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}k}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2/5}\|f\langle v\rangle^{-k}\|_{H^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{x}}^{3/5}\lesssim\|f\langle v\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}k}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|f\langle v\rangle^{-k}\|_{H^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}},

also we have

(31)

\|f\|_{L^{3}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{1/2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\|f\langle v\rangle^{k}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|f\langle v\rangle^{-k}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}.

3.
Linearized and nonlinear estimate for the Boltzmann operator

In this chapter we will prove linearized and nonlinear estimate for the collision operator
Q
.

Lemma 3.1
.

For any
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, for any
k\geq 4
,
h,g
smooth, we have

\displaystyle|(Q(h,\mu),g\langle v\rangle^{2k})|\leq

\displaystyle\|b(\cos\theta)\sin^{k-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}\|_{L^{1}_{\theta}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}\|g\|_{{L}^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}+C_{k}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|b(\cos\theta)\sin^{k-2}\frac{\theta}{2}\|_{L^{1}_{\theta}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}\|g\|_{{L}^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}+C_{k}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}},

for some constant
C_{k}>0
.

Proof.

We first compute
Q^{+}
, by pre-post collisional change of variables and Lemma
2.7
we have

\displaystyle(Q^{+}(h,\mu),g\langle v\rangle^{2k})=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)h(v_{*})\mu(v)g(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2k}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{k}\frac{\theta}{2}h(v_{*})\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k}\mu(v)g(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)h(v_{*})\mu(v)g(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}R_{1}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)h(v_{*})\mu(v)g(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}R_{2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma:=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}+\Gamma_{3},

where

|R_{1}|\leq C_{k}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k-1}\langle v\rangle,\quad|R_{2}|\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{k}.

For the
\Gamma_{1}
term, by the first inequality of Lemma
2.2

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{k-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|h_{*}|^{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2k}\mu dvdv_{*}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\mu|g^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2k}dvdv^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{k-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\|h\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}.

For the
\Gamma_{2}
term, since
\frac{3+\gamma}{2}\leq 2
, by Lemma
2.2
we have

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{2}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|h_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k-1}\mu\langle v\rangle|g^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|h_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k-1/2}\mu\langle v\rangle^{2}|g^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k-1/2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|h_{*}|^{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2k-1}\langle v\rangle^{2}\mu dvdv_{*}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\langle v\rangle^{2}\mu|g^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2k-1}dvdv^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}}.

For the
\Gamma_{3}
term, since
k-1\geq 3
, by Lemma
2.7
we have

\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k-1}\leq C_{k}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k-1}+C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{k-1},

thus we split
\Gamma_{3}
into

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{3}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)|h_{*}|\mu\langle v\rangle^{k}|g^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|h_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k-1}\mu\langle v\rangle^{k}|g^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|h_{*}|\mu\langle v\rangle^{2k-1}|g^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle dvdv_{*}d\sigma:=\Gamma_{31}+\Gamma_{32},

the
\Gamma_{31}
term can be proved the same way as the
\Gamma_{2}
term. For
\Gamma_{32}
term, by the regular change of variables and Lemma
2.8
if
k\geq 4
we have

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{32}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|h_{*}|\mu\langle v\rangle^{2k-1}|g^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|h_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{1/2}\mu\langle v\rangle^{2k-1/2}|g^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{1/2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\cos^{-(3+\gamma)}\frac{\theta}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v^{\prime}-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|h_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{1/2}|g^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{1/2}dv^{\prime}dv_{*}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}},

the
Q^{+}
term is thus proved. For the
Q^{-}
part by Lemma
2.8
we have

\displaystyle|(Q^{-}(h,\mu),g\langle\cdot\rangle^{2k})|=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)h(v_{*})\mu(v)g(v)\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)|h(v_{*})||g(v)|dvdv_{*}d\sigma\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}},

the theorem is thus proved by combing the
Q^{+}
and
Q^{-}
term.
∎

Lemma 3.2
.

Suppose that
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, then for any smooth function
f
we have

\displaystyle(Q(\mu,f),f\langle v\rangle^{2k})\leq

\displaystyle-\|b(\cos\theta)(1-\cos^{k-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2})\|_{L^{1}_{\theta}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}}^{2},

for some constant
C_{k}>0
. In particular if
k\geq 4
we have

\displaystyle(Q(\mu,f),f\langle v\rangle^{2k})\leq

\displaystyle-\|b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\|_{L^{1}_{\theta}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}}^{2}.

Proof.

We first compute
Q^{+}
, by pre-post collisional change of variables and Lemma
2.7

we have

\displaystyle(Q^{+}(\mu,f),f\langle v\rangle^{2k})=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)\mu(v_{*})f(v)f(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2k}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)\cos^{k}\frac{\theta}{2}\mu(v_{*})f(v)\langle v\rangle^{k}f(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)\mu(v_{*})f(v)g(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}Q_{1}dvdv_{*}d\sigma:=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2},

where
|Q_{1}|\leq C_{k}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k}\langle v\rangle^{k-1}
.
For the
\Gamma_{1}
term, by the second inequality of Lemma
2.2
we have

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\cos^{k-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu(v_{*})|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdv_{*}.

For the
\Gamma_{2}
term, we compute

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{2}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu_{*}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k}|f|\langle v\rangle^{k-1}|f^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu_{*}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k+1/2}|f|\langle v\rangle^{k-1/2}|f^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k-1/2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\cos^{-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k+1/2}\mu_{*}|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2k-1}dvdv_{*}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k+1/2}\mu_{*}|f^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2k-1}dvdv^{\prime}\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-1/2}}^{2}.

It is easily seen that

(-Q^{-}(\mu,f),f\langle v\rangle^{2k})=-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)d\sigma\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu(v_{*})|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdv_{*},

so the proof is finished by gathering the three terms.
∎

Then we come to estimate the nonlinear operator
Q^{+}
.

Lemma 3.3
.

For any
\gamma\in(-3,1]
, for smooth function
f,g,h
, recall
N
is defined in (
8
), for any
k\geq 4
we have

|(Q^{+}(f,g),h\langle v\rangle^{2k})|\leq C_{k}\min_{m+n=N-2}\{\|f\|_{H^{m}_{k+\gamma/2}}\|g\|_{H^{n}_{4}}\}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}}+C_{k}\min_{m_{1}+n_{1}=N-2}\{\|g\|_{H^{m_{1}}_{k+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{H^{n_{1}}_{4}}\}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}},

where
m,n,m_{1},n_{1}
are nonnegative integers.

Proof.

We first prove the case
-\frac{3}{2}<\gamma\leq 1
, we have
